# Management Accounting for Engineers: question papers in full

110 unique papers · 2021-22 to 2025-26 · Data Science, Computer Engineering, Common (all branches), Information Technology, Artificial Intelligence, Electronics and Telecommunication, Mechanical, Mechatronics, Civil, Cyber Security, Computer Science and Business Systems, AI and Data Science, AI and Machine Learning

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

Apply
• Annual Demand = 3,600 units
• Ordering Cost = ₹ 120 per order
• Purchase Price = ₹ 60 per unit
• Annual Carrying Cost = 25% of the unit price
The supplier offers a trade discount of 5% on the purchase price if the order size is 800
units or more.
Required:
1. Calculate the Economic Order Quantity (EOQ) at the regular price.
2. Compute the total annual inventory-related cost (Purchase Cost + Ordering Cost +
Carrying Cost) at EOQ and at an order size of 800 units (with discount).
3. Advise whether the company should avail the quantity discount. State the net annual
saving, if any.

Fixed Selling Expenses   Fixed   1,80,000
Distribution Overheads (50% Fixed)   Semi-variable   1,20,000
i. Raw material cost per unit will increase by 5% when production reaches 80% capacity
and above.
ii. Direct labour cost per unit will increase by 8% when capacity reaches 80% capacity and
above.
iii. Variable factory overheads per unit will decrease by 4% at 100% capacity (efficiency
gains).
iv. Selling price = ₹ 200 per unit; a discount of 2% will be allowed on all sales at 100%
capacity.
v. Variable selling expenses will increase by ₹ 2 per unit when capacity reaches 80% and
above.
vi. An additional fixed overhead of ₹ 80,000 will be incurred at 100% capacity.

ii. Direct labour cost per unit will increase by 10% when capacity exceeds
75%.

exceeds 85% capacity (efficiency gains).
iv. Selling price = ₹ 200 per unit; a discount of 3% will be allowed at 80%
capacity and above.
v. Variable selling expenses will increase by ₹ 3 per unit when capacity
reaches 80% and above.
vi. An additional fixed overhead of ₹ 60,000 will be incurred at 100% capacity.

The company will then increase its dividend by 10 percent per year for two years

after which it will maintain a constant 4 percent dividend growth rate. What is one

share worth today at a required rate of return of 13 percent?

The dividends for Pulaski Corp. are projected at $2.00, $2.50, and $3.00 over the
next three years, respectively. After that, the dividends are expected to increase by

(5]
5 percent annually. What is one share of this stock worth at a required rate of return
of 15 percent?
